# 15. Sustainable Construction Policy

## 1. Introduction

- Higher Education Institutions (HEIs) are major consumers of energy, water, and natural resources, making sustainability in construction a priority.

- This policy provides a framework for planning, designing, constructing, and maintaining campus infrastructure with minimal environmental impact.

- It aligns with NAAC (Criterion 4 & 7), NBA (Criterion 7), NIRF (TLR & OI), QS I·GAUGE (Green Campus, Sustainability), NEP-2020, and UN SDGs (7, 9, 11, 12, 13).

## 2. Objectives

- Promote the use of eco-friendly and green-certified materials in all new constructions and renovations.

- Ensure integration of renewable energy sources (solar, wind, biomass) into campus facilities.

- Adopt smart building technologies for energy efficiency, water conservation, and occupant safety.

- Create a climate-resilient campus that reduces carbon footprint and operational costs.

- Establish a system of monitoring, compliance, and accreditation readiness.

## 3. Scope

**Applies to:**

- New constructions (academic blocks, hostels, libraries, labs, admin buildings).

- Renovations and retrofitting projects.

- Campus-wide infrastructure upgrades (lighting, HVAC, transport, ICT-enabled facilities).

## 4. Policy Guidelines

### A. Green Materials

- **Use low-carbon footprint materials:** fly-ash bricks, recycled steel, bamboo, recycled aggregates.

- Ensure paints, adhesives, and finishes are low-VOC (Volatile Organic Compounds).

- Procure locally sourced materials to reduce transportation emissions.

- Mandate certified green materials (IGBC, GRIHA, LEED).

### B. Renewable Energy Integration

- Install rooftop solar plants on academic, hostel, and admin buildings.

- Use solar water heating in hostels and canteens.

- Promote energy-efficient HVAC systems powered by renewable sources.

- Integrate biogas plants for hostel/canteen waste and solar EV charging stations.

### C. Smart Building Technologies

- Deploy IoT-based energy monitoring systems (smart meters, automated lighting).

- Install motion-sensor lighting and smart HVAC controls.

- Integrate Building Management Systems (BMS) for central monitoring.

- Use rainwater harvesting sensors and smart irrigation systems for landscaping.

### D. Water & Waste Management

- Mandatory rainwater harvesting in all new projects.

- Greywater treatment and reuse for landscaping and flushing.

- Campus-wide segregation of waste (biodegradable, recyclable, e-waste).

- Tie-up with certified vendors for e-waste and biomedical waste disposal.

### E. Building Design & Certification

- All new projects must target Green Building Certification (IGBC/GRIHA/LEED).

- Designs should maximize natural ventilation, daylighting, and passive cooling.

- Ensure disaster resilience (seismic safety, fire compliance, flood prevention).

## 5. Governance & Responsibility

- **Campus Development Committee (CDC):** Oversees implementation.

- **Green Campus Committee:** Monitors sustainability aspects.

- **Engineering & Facilities Department:** Ensures procurement and compliance.

- **IQAC:** Maintains accreditation and audit evidence.

## 6. Monitoring & Compliance

- Maintain a Sustainable Construction Compliance Register (materials, energy, water, waste, smart systems).

- Conduct quarterly audits of ongoing projects.

- Include sustainability indicators in Monthly Campus Development Reports.

- Document certifications, procurement records, and vendor compliance.

## 7. Accreditation & Quality Linkages

- NAAC Criterion 4 – Green Campus & Infrastructure adequacy.

- NBA Criterion 7 – Facilities & technical support with sustainability.

- NIRF (TLR, OI) – Emphasis on green and inclusive infrastructure.

- QS I·GAUGE – Green Campus, Governance, Sustainability metrics.

- **SDGs:**

<!-- -->

- **SDG 7:** Affordable & Clean Energy.

- **SDG 9:** Industry, Innovation, Infrastructure.

- **SDG 11:** Sustainable Cities & Communities.

- **SDG 12:** Responsible Consumption & Production.

- **SDG 13:** Climate Action.

## 8. Review & Upgradation

- This policy will be reviewed every 3 years or after completion of any major infrastructure project.

- Updates will incorporate emerging sustainable technologies (AI-driven energy optimization, green hydrogen, carbon-neutral designs).

# 16. Annual Maintenance Contract (AMC) Policy

## 1. Introduction

- Reliable infrastructure and ICT systems are essential for smooth academic, administrative, and research activities in HEIs.

- To ensure safety, functionality, and longevity of assets, the institution adopts an Annual Maintenance Contract (AMC) Policy.

- This policy ensures timely servicing of equipment, transparent vendor management, and audit-ready documentation for NAAC, NBA, NIRF, QS I·GAUGE, UGC, AICTE, and ABET compliance.

## 2. Objectives

- Maintain all IT systems, laboratory equipment, electrical, and physical facilities in optimal condition.

- Minimize breakdowns and downtime through preventive maintenance.

- Ensure compliance with statutory and accreditation norms (safety, ICT, accessibility, sustainability).

- Extend the useful life of assets while controlling repair/replacement costs.

- Create a culture of accountability and documentation in facility management.

## 3. Scope

**Applies to all equipment, ICT resources, and facilities, including:**

- **ICT & IT Systems:** Servers, desktops, laptops, UPS, projectors, printers, network equipment.

- **Laboratory Equipment:** Scientific instruments, simulators, technical lab setups.

- **Electrical & Mechanical Systems:** Generators, transformers, elevators, air conditioners, motors.

- **Physical Facilities:** Smart classrooms, seminar halls, libraries, hostels, canteens, sports complexes.

- **Green & Sustainable Infra:** Solar plants, rainwater harvesting systems, water treatment, waste management units.

## 4. Types of AMC

- **Comprehensive AMC (CAMC):** Covers all parts, labor, replacements, and services.

- **Non-Comprehensive AMC (NCAMC):** Covers only labor/service charges; parts replaced are billed separately.

- **Hybrid/Customized AMC:** Combination of CAMC and NCAMC depending on criticality of equipment.

## 5. Policy Guidelines

### A. Vendor Selection

- Vendors must be authorized service providers or certified by OEM (Original Equipment Manufacturer).

- Selection through transparent tendering/quotation process.

- Preference to vendors with experience in servicing educational institutions.

### B. AMC Duration & Renewal

- AMC will typically be 1–3 years, renewable annually.

- Renewal subject to performance review, compliance reports, and user feedback.

### C. Preventive & Corrective Maintenance

- **Preventive Maintenance Schedule (PMS):** Vendors must follow fixed schedules (monthly/quarterly/annual).

- **Corrective Maintenance:** Issues must be attended within 24–48 hours of complaint logging.

- Emergency response for critical systems (servers, power, labs).

### D. Compliance & Safety

- **All AMC activities must comply with:**

<!-- -->

- NAAC Criterion 4 & 7 (Infrastructure, Institutional Values).

- AICTE/UGC requirements for labs, ICT, and accessibility.

- NBA Criterion 7 (Facilities & Technical Support).

- ABET Criteria 7 (Adequacy & safety of facilities).

<!-- -->

- Safety checks (fire alarms, electrical earthing, elevators, lab safety) must be integrated into AMC.

### E. Documentation & Reporting

- **AMC Register to be maintained with:**

<!-- -->

- Equipment details, vendor details, AMC period.

- Maintenance schedules, service reports, breakdown history.

<!-- -->

- Monthly AMC Audit Reports to be submitted to IQAC/CDC.

- Records to be preserved for minimum 5 years as accreditation evidence.

## 6. Roles & Responsibilities

- **IT Cell / Lab In-Charges:** Identify equipment requiring AMC, supervise vendor servicing.

- **Engineering / Facilities Dept.:** Manage contracts for civil, electrical, and utilities.

- **Finance Dept.:** Ensure timely payment as per contract milestones.

- **IQAC / CDC:** Review AMC compliance reports, integrate into accreditation documentation.

## 7. Monitoring & Review

- Quarterly review of AMC performance by Campus Development Committee (CDC).

- Vendor performance ratings to decide renewal or replacement.

- Annual consolidated AMC compliance report to Governing Body/IQAC.

## 8. Benefits

- Reduced downtime of critical academic and research infrastructure.

- Extended asset lifecycle, lower replacement costs.

- Safety, sustainability, and compliance with accreditation standards.

- Transparent vendor management and accountability.

- Strong documentation support for NAAC, NBA, ABET, and NIRF evidence folders.

# 17. Capital Expenditure & Budget Allocation Policy

## 1. Introduction

- Capital expenditure (CapEx) in HEIs refers to long-term investments in physical infrastructure, ICT facilities, laboratories, research centers, and sustainable campus projects.

- This policy ensures that budget allocation, approval, utilization, and monitoring of capital funds are carried out with transparency, accountability, and alignment with institutional goals.

- It supports compliance with NAAC Criterion 4, NBA Criterion 7, UGC/AICTE norms, NIRF TLR, and SDG 9 & 11.

## 2. Objectives

- To establish a structured framework for capital investment decisions.

- To align capital expenditure with the strategic plan and master campus development plan.

- To prioritize funding for academic, research, student-centric, and sustainability projects.

- To ensure cost efficiency, transparency, and audit readiness.

- To maintain compliance with government, statutory, and accreditation standards.

## 3. Scope

**Covers all capital investment areas such as:**

- **Academic Facilities:** Classrooms, laboratories, libraries, research centers.

- **Student Amenities:** Hostels, canteens, sports complexes, health centers.

- **Administrative Infrastructure:** Department offices, exam cells, governance facilities.

- **Digital Infrastructure:** Data centers, ERP, LMS, smart classrooms, campus Wi-Fi.

- **Utilities & Services:** Power, water, sanitation, transport, landscaping.

- **Sustainability Projects:** Solar plants, rainwater harvesting, waste management, green buildings.

## 4. Policy Guidelines

### A. Budget Planning

- Annual CapEx budget to be prepared by Finance Office in consultation with:

<!-- -->

- Campus Development Committee (CDC).

- IT Cell, Library, Hostel & Lab Committees.

- Green Campus Committee.

<!-- -->

- Align with Institutional Strategic Plan, NEP 2020 reforms, and Accreditation needs.

### B. Approval Mechanism

- Departmental heads submit proposals with justifications.

- Proposals vetted by CDC and Finance Committee.

- Final approval by Governing Body/Board of Management.

- **Priority given to:**

<!-- -->

- Statutory compliance projects (fire safety, accessibility).

- Accreditation readiness (ICT upgrades, labs, libraries).

- Sustainability (solar, rainwater, smart infrastructure).

### C. Allocation Principles

- **Academic Facilities:** ≥ 40% of CapEx budget.

- **Research & Innovation:** ≥ 15%.

- **Student Amenities & Welfare:** ≥ 15%.

- **Digital Infrastructure:** ≥ 10%.

- **Sustainability & Green Projects:** ≥ 10%.

- **Contingency & Miscellaneous:** ≤ 10%.

### D. Procurement & Utilization

- Follow transparent procurement process (tendering, quotations, vendor empanelment).

- Preference for Make in India and eco-friendly products.

- Digital record of utilization with invoices and audit trails.

### E. Monitoring & Review

- Quarterly review by CDC and IQAC.

- Annual consolidated Capital Expenditure Audit Report submitted to Governing Body.

- Variances between allocated and actual expenditure to be documented with justifications.

## 5. Roles & Responsibilities

- **Departments / Committees:** Identify needs, prepare proposals.

- **Finance Office:** Budget preparation, fund allocation, monitoring.

- **CDC:** Technical feasibility, alignment with master plan.

- **IQAC:** Accreditation compliance and documentation.

- **Governing Body:** Final approval, policy oversight.

## 6. Compliance & Accreditation Linkages

- NAAC Criterion 4 & 6 – Adequacy of physical and digital infrastructure, financial resource mobilization.

- NBA Criterion 7 – Facilities & technical support adequacy.

- NIRF TLR – Capital investment in teaching-learning resources.

- UGC / AICTE Norms – Minimum facilities in labs, classrooms, libraries, hostels.

- QS I·GAUGE – Infrastructure, Governance, Sustainability.

- ABET Criterion 7 – Adequacy and maintenance of facilities.

- SDGs 9, 11, 12, 13 – Infrastructure, sustainable cities, responsible consumption, climate action.

## 7. Review of Policy

- This policy will be reviewed annually during financial planning.

- Major revisions every 3 years or based on regulatory changes.

# 18. Budget Plan – Procurement of Stationery, Inventory, and Construction

## 1. Objectives

- Ensure systematic allocation of funds for academic and administrative needs.

- Maintain transparency & accountability in procurement.

- Align budget utilization with NAAC Criterion 4 & 6, NBA Criterion 7, UGC/AICTE norms, NIRF TLR, and SDG 12 (responsible consumption).

- Provide evidence-ready documentation for audits and accreditation.

## 2. Budget Categories

### A. Stationery Procurement

- Printing paper (A4, A3, legal).

- Office supplies (pens, pencils, markers, files, folders).

- Examination stationery (answer sheets, attendance registers, OMR sheets).

- Teaching-learning stationery (lab notebooks, graph sheets, chart papers).

- Digital stationery (printer cartridges, toners, CDs, DVDs, USBs).

### B. Inventory Procurement

- ICT consumables (network cables, UPS batteries, projectors, peripherals).

- Laboratory consumables (chemicals, glassware, reagents).

- Library inventory (newspapers, journals, binding materials).

- Housekeeping supplies (cleaning material, sanitizers, toiletries).

- Furniture and fixture spares (chairs, tables, repair kits).

### C. Construction & Infrastructure

- Civil works (new classrooms, labs, hostels, offices).

- Renovation/repairs (painting, flooring, roofing, electrical rewiring).

- Green campus construction (rainwater harvesting, solar panel structures, compost pits).

- Safety compliance works (fire extinguishers, ramps, signage, emergency exits).

- Digital infra construction (data center rooms, smart classroom wiring).

# 19. Accreditation Compliance Policy

## 1. Introduction

- Accreditation assures quality, accountability, and global recognition of an institution’s teaching, research, and infrastructure.

- HEIs must maintain continuous compliance with regulatory and accreditation requirements rather than treating them as periodic exercises.

- This policy establishes a systematic framework for ensuring compliance, documentation, and audit readiness.

## 2. Objectives

- To embed accreditation standards into institutional governance.

- To ensure continuous quality improvement across teaching, research, infrastructure, and student services.

- To maintain evidence-ready documentation for accreditation bodies.

- To align HEI functions with NEP 2020, SDGs, and Washington Accord (ABET/NBA equivalence).

- To strengthen the institution’s national ranking (NIRF) and international benchmarking (QS, Times Higher Ed).

## 3. Scope

**Applies to all areas of HEIs:**

- Academic – Curriculum design, teaching, learning outcomes, research output.

- Infrastructure – ICT facilities, labs, hostels, green campus initiatives.

- Governance – Finance, HR, administrative processes, leadership.

- Student Services – Mentoring, placements, welfare, accessibility.

- Sustainability – Green initiatives, renewable energy, waste management.

## 4. Policy Guidelines

### A. Alignment with Accreditation Bodies

- NAAC → Focus on holistic institutional quality (7 Criteria).

- NBA → Program-level quality and outcome-based education (OBE).

- UGC/AICTE → Statutory requirements for recognition and technical programs.

- NIRF → Annual ranking performance based on TLR, GO, OI, RP, PR.

- QS I·GAUGE → Global benchmarking (teaching, employability, governance, sustainability).

- ABET → Program-level international standard in engineering/technology.

### B. Accreditation Documentation System

- **Maintain Registers & Reports for:**

<!-- -->

- ICT Facilities

- Physical Facilities & Maintenance

- AMC Compliance

- Capital Expenditure & Budget Allocation

- Green Campus, Accessibility, Space Utilization

- Teaching-Learning Outcomes (CO-PO-PSO Mapping)

<!-- -->

- Ensure digital storage in accreditation evidence folders (IQAC-managed).

### C. Governance & Review

- **Accreditation Compliance Committee (ACC):**

<!-- -->

- **Chair:** Dean (Accreditation/Quality)

- **Members:** IQAC Coordinator, HoDs, Librarian, IT Head, Finance Officer, Student Representative

<!-- -->

- **Responsibilities:**

<!-- -->

- Monitor ongoing compliance.

- Maintain yearly accreditation readiness index.

- Conduct mock audits every semester.

- Submit Quarterly Accreditation Compliance Report to Governing Body.

### D. Training & Awareness

- **Faculty and staff trained in:**

<!-- -->

- OBE practices (NBA/ABET)

- Documentation for NAAC/NIRF/UGC

- Green Campus & Sustainability Evidence

<!-- -->

- Students made aware of accreditation benefits (placements, international mobility).

### E. Monitoring & Continuous Improvement

- Quarterly internal audits by IQAC.

- Corrective & preventive actions for non-compliance.

- Feedback loops → student surveys, faculty appraisals, alumni & employer feedback.

- Annual Accreditation Review Workshop with stakeholders.

## 5. Roles & Responsibilities

- IQAC – Custodian of documentation, audits, and evidence records.

- Departments – Maintain program-level records (CO-PO attainment, labs, teaching).

- Finance & Facilities – Budget, CapEx, AMC, infrastructure compliance.

- IT Cell – ICT facilities, ERP, e-learning compliance.

- Green Campus Committee – Sustainability compliance (SDGs, QS I·GAUGE).

- Governing Body – Policy approval, funding allocation, oversight.

## 6. Accreditation Linkages

- NAAC Criterion 1–7 – Curriculum, Teaching, Research, Infrastructure, Student Support, Governance, Best Practices.

- NBA Criterion 1–7 – Program Educational Objectives (PEOs), Program Outcomes (POs), Continuous Improvement.

- NIRF – Ranking metrics (TLR, RP, GO, OI, PR).

- UGC/AICTE – Statutory compliance (labs, classrooms, student-faculty ratio, ICT).

- QS I·GAUGE – Global benchmarking (Facilities, Governance, Employability, Diversity).

- ABET – OBE framework, program outcomes, continuous quality improvement.

- SDGs – Sustainable campuses, inclusivity, climate action.

## 7. Monitoring & Review

- Accreditation compliance will be monitored quarterly by ACC.

- An Annual Accreditation Compliance Report will be presented to the Governing Body.

- The policy will be reviewed every 3 years or earlier if regulatory norms change.

# 20. Waste Management & Recycling Policy

## 1. Introduction

- HEIs generate significant solid, liquid, electronic, biomedical, and hazardous waste through academic, research, administrative, and residential activities.

- **Proper management and recycling of waste is essential to:**

<!-- -->

- Promote a clean, green, and sustainable campus.

- Ensure compliance with government regulations (Solid Waste Management Rules 2016, E-Waste Rules, Bio-Medical Waste Rules).

- Demonstrate institutional commitment to NAAC/NBA criteria, NIRF inclusivity, QS I·GAUGE Green Campus, and SDGs (11, 12, 13).

## 2. Objectives

- To adopt a zero-waste campus approach.

- To implement effective segregation, collection, disposal, and recycling mechanisms.

- To encourage the use of eco-friendly alternatives over single-use plastics.

- To engage students and staff in awareness and participation in sustainable waste practices.

- To establish documentation and monitoring systems for accreditation and compliance.

## 3. Scope

**Applies to all waste generated across:**

- **Academic spaces:** Classrooms, laboratories, libraries.

- **Research facilities:** Science labs, workshops, innovation centers.

- **Administrative areas:** Offices, exam cell, IT centers.

- **Student amenities:** Hostels, canteens, sports complexes.

- **Medical facilities:** Campus health centers.

- **Digital/ICT waste:** Servers, computers, peripherals, e-waste.

## 4. Policy Guidelines

### A. Solid Waste Management

- Mandatory segregation at source (biodegradable, recyclable, hazardous).

- Installation of color-coded bins across campus.

- Biodegradable waste sent to composting units/biogas plants.

- Recyclables (paper, plastics, metals) sold to authorized recyclers.

- Non-recyclables sent to municipal collection as per rules.

### B. Liquid Waste Management

- Wastewater from hostels, canteens, and labs treated in STPs (Sewage Treatment Plants).

- Treated water reused for landscaping and flushing.

- Chemical lab waste neutralized before disposal.

### C. E-Waste Management

- Inventory of all ICT assets maintained by IT Cell.

- Obsolete/destructive ICT equipment sent to authorized e-waste recyclers.

- Reuse and redistribution of functional ICT equipment within campus.

### D. Biomedical Waste Management

- Waste from health centers segregated as per Bio-Medical Waste Rules.

- Sharps, expired medicines, and contaminated waste disposed of via authorized biomedical waste handlers.

### E. Hazardous Waste Management

- Hazardous lab chemicals stored and neutralized in controlled environments.

- Proper training provided to lab staff for handling.

- Safe storage areas maintained as per regulatory standards.

### F. Awareness & Capacity Building

- Regular workshops on waste segregation and recycling.

- Student-led green clubs for campus waste audits.

- Competitions and campaigns to promote plastic-free and paperless campus.

### G. Monitoring & Documentation

- Waste Management Register maintained (daily collection, disposal, recycling data).

- Monthly Waste Management Report generated by Green Campus Committee.

- Annual Green Audit by third-party agencies.

## 5. Roles & Responsibilities

- **Green Campus Committee:** Overall monitoring and policy enforcement.

- **Engineering & Facilities Dept.:** Implementation of STPs, composting, collection logistics.

- **IT Cell:** E-waste monitoring and disposal.

- **Health Center:** Biomedical waste management compliance.

- **IQAC:** Accreditation documentation and evidence folders.

## 6. Accreditation & Quality Linkages

- NAAC Criterion 7 – Institutional Values & Best Practices (Green Campus, waste management).

- NBA Criterion 7 – Facilities & Technical Support (lab safety, waste disposal).

- NIRF OI – Inclusivity & sustainability practices.

- QS I·GAUGE – Green Campus rating.

- SDGs – SDG 11 (Sustainable Cities), SDG 12 (Responsible Consumption), SDG 13 (Climate Action).

## 7. Review & Upgradation

- Policy reviewed every 2 years by Green Campus Committee.

- Updated with new sustainability technologies (AI-based waste tracking, IoT smart bins, circular economy practices).

# 21. Energy & Utilities Management Policy

## 1. Introduction

- Energy and utility services are the lifeline of academic, research, and residential operations in HEIs.

- Efficient management reduces costs, carbon footprint, and risks of outages, while aligning with global sustainability practices.

- This policy integrates sustainable energy use, water conservation, and utility efficiency into institutional governance.

## 2. Objectives

- Optimize the use of electricity, water, and HVAC utilities across campus.

- Increase adoption of renewable energy sources (solar, wind, biogas).

- Reduce the environmental impact of energy and utility consumption.

- Ensure compliance with national standards (Energy Conservation Act, Water Act, Pollution Control Guidelines).

- Provide evidence of sustainability practices for accreditation (NAAC, NBA, NIRF, QS I·GAUGE, SDGs).

## 3. Scope

Applies to all academic, administrative, student, and research facilities, including:

- Academic blocks (classrooms, labs, libraries, research centers).

- Administrative offices, examination cells.

- Hostels, canteens, health centers, sports complexes.

- IT/Data centers, smart classrooms.

- **Utilities:** water supply, sanitation, HVAC systems, power backup.

- Green & renewable energy infrastructure (solar plants, rainwater harvesting, STPs).

## 4. Policy Guidelines

### A. Energy Management

- Install energy-efficient appliances (LED lighting, star-rated ACs, smart meters).

- Promote solar rooftop systems and biogas plants.

- Conduct energy audits annually; implement recommendations.

- Enforce shutdown policies (switch off labs, classrooms, and offices after hours).

- Monitor energy use via IoT-enabled smart meters and dashboards.

### B. Water Management

- Install water meters for all hostels, labs, and blocks.

- Promote rainwater harvesting across campus.

- Recycle wastewater through STPs (Sewage Treatment Plants) and reuse for gardening and flushing.

- Promote water conservation campaigns (drip irrigation, leak monitoring).

### C. HVAC & Cooling Systems

- Use centralized HVAC with automation for efficiency.

- Regular maintenance of chillers, AC units, and ventilation.

- Set temperature standards to reduce energy load.

- Install sensor-based controls in classrooms and labs.

### D. Utilities & Power Backup

- Maintain generators, UPS, and battery systems with AMC.

- Ensure redundancy in power supply to avoid outages during exams/research.

- Establish disaster recovery plans for ICT and data centers.

### E. Renewable & Green Practices

- Allocate 10% of annual CapEx for renewable energy.

- Develop solar, wind, and biomass projects to reduce grid dependency.

- Promote paperless governance (ERP, LMS, digital records).

- Integrate sustainability into curriculum & research.

## 5. Monitoring & Compliance

- Green Campus Committee to oversee policy implementation.

- Monthly Energy & Utilities Report to be prepared by Facilities & IT Cell.

- **Registers to be maintained:**

<!-- -->

- Energy Audit Register

- Water Usage Register

- Renewable Energy Production Register

- AMC & Maintenance Compliance Register

<!-- -->

- Annual Green Audit by third-party agencies.

## 6. Roles & Responsibilities

- **Facilities Dept.:** Energy audits, AMC compliance, HVAC maintenance.

- **Green Campus Committee:** Renewable projects, awareness drives.

- **IT Cell:** Smart metering, energy dashboards, ICT power management.

- **IQAC:** Accreditation documentation and compliance monitoring.

- **Finance Dept.:** Allocate CapEx/OpEx for sustainable utilities.

## 7. Accreditation & Standards Alignment

- NAAC Criterion 4 & 7 – Infrastructure, sustainability, institutional values.

- NBA Criterion 7 – Facilities, utilities, technical support.

- AICTE/UGC Norms – Minimum facilities, green campus requirements.

- NIRF (OI) – Sustainability & outreach practices.

- QS I·GAUGE – Green Campus & Energy Efficiency indicators.

- ABET Criterion 7 – Facilities adequacy, safety, sustainability.

- SDGs 6, 7, 11, 12, 13 – Water management, clean energy, sustainable campuses, climate action.

## 8. Review & Improvement

- Policy to be reviewed every 2 years or as per regulatory updates.

- **Key Performance Indicators (KPIs):**

<!-- -->

- Energy saved (kWh reduction).

- Renewable energy contribution (%).

- Water recycled & reused (Litres).

- Cost savings achieved.

- Accreditation compliance scores improved.

# 22. Standard List of Housekeeping Activities in HEIs

## 1. Daily Activities

- Classrooms & Laboratories

<!-- -->

- Sweeping and mopping of floors.

- Dusting of desks, benches, chairs, and lab equipment.

- Emptying waste bins and replacing liners.

- Cleaning whiteboards/blackboards.

- Checking and cleaning projector screens, ICT equipment.

<!-- -->

- Administrative & Office Areas

<!-- -->

- Dusting of furniture, filing cabinets, and office equipment.

- Cleaning telephones, computers, and printers.

- Emptying dustbins and shredders.

<!-- -->

- Toilets & Washrooms

<!-- -->

- Cleaning floors, walls, and sanitary fixtures.

- Refilling soap dispensers, tissue rolls, and hand dryers.

- Disinfecting urinals and wash basins.

<!-- -->

- Common Areas

<!-- -->

- Sweeping/mopping corridors, staircases, and lobbies.

- Cleaning elevators and railings.

- Sanitizing frequently touched surfaces (switches, handles, doorknobs).

<!-- -->

- Hostels & Residential Areas

<!-- -->

- Bed-making and room cleaning in student hostels.

- Cleaning bathrooms and common kitchens.

- Garbage collection and disposal.

## 2. Weekly Activities

- Dusting and wiping fans, light fittings, and electrical panels.

- Polishing wooden furniture, doors, and windows.

- Washing curtains, cushion covers, and mats.

- Scrubbing washrooms with anti-scaling cleaners.

- Deep cleaning hostel mess/canteen kitchens.

- Cleaning library racks, computer labs, and seminar halls thoroughly.

## 3. Monthly Activities

- Machine scrubbing of large floor areas (library, auditorium, labs).

- Polishing of metal fittings and fixtures.

- Pest control measures in classrooms, labs, and hostels.

- Checking plumbing systems (leaks, taps, drainage).

- Washing water coolers and purifiers.

- Cleaning rooftops, terraces, and rainwater harvesting tanks.

- Deep cleaning hostel blocks, canteens, and guest houses.

## 4. Quarterly Activities

- Shampooing and vacuuming of carpets and rugs.

- Cleaning external facades of buildings and windows.

- Disinfection of water storage tanks.

- Deep cleaning of HVAC ducts and air filters.

- Oil and polish of wooden furniture and fixtures.

- Maintenance of sports grounds and gymnasiums.

## 5. Annual Activities

- Whitewashing/painting of classrooms, hostels, and labs (if required).

- Polishing of marble/stone flooring.

- Comprehensive pest control for the entire campus.

- Overhaul cleaning of auditoriums, seminar halls, and conference centers.

- Annual sustainability audit for waste management and green campus compliance.

## 6. Specialized Housekeeping Activities

- **ICT Equipment Cleaning:** Proper dust-removal of projectors, servers, UPS systems.

- **Green Campus Maintenance:** Tree plantation, lawn mowing, cleaning compost pits.

- **Safety & Hygiene:** Fire extinguisher cleaning, sanitizing medical facilities, ensuring accessibility pathways are clutter-free.

- **Event Management:** Pre- and post-event cleaning of seminar halls, auditoriums, sports facilities.

## 7. Accreditation & Standards Linkage

- NAAC Criterion 4 & 7 → Infrastructure & Institutional Values.

- NBA Criterion 7 → Facilities and Technical Support.

- AICTE/UGC Norms → Minimum hygiene and campus facilities standards.

- NIRF (TLR & OI) → Teaching-learning infrastructure & inclusivity.

- QS I·GAUGE → Facilities, Green Campus, Hygiene Standards.

- SDG 3, 6, 11 → Health & well-being, clean water, sustainable cities.

# 23. Standard List of Activities on Solar Panels in HEIs

## 1. Daily / Routine Activities

- Visual inspection of panels for dust, debris, bird droppings, or shading.

- Check inverter display for power generation readings.

- Record daily generation data in the Solar Energy Register.

- Ensure no obstruction (new construction, overgrown trees) is affecting sunlight.

- Verify indicator lights on inverter and control panels.

## 2. Weekly Activities

- Cleaning of panel surfaces with soft cloths/water spray (especially in dusty environments).

- Checking cables, connectors, and junction boxes for loose or exposed wiring.

- Monitoring string performance using data loggers.

- Confirm earthing points are intact and not corroded.

## 3. Monthly Activities

- Performance ratio (PR) analysis – compare expected vs. actual energy generation.

- Inspection of mounting structures for rust, cracks, or instability.

- Testing DC/AC voltage and current output.

- Checking inverter efficiency logs and error codes.

- Inspecting protection devices (MCBs, fuses, surge protectors).

- Clean and verify monitoring system dashboards (SCADA/IoT).

## 4. Quarterly Activities

- Detailed thermographic scan of panels for hotspots.

- Cleaning and greasing of mechanical parts (if panels are on trackers).

- Verify battery health (for hybrid/off-grid systems).

- Water quality check (to ensure no mineral deposits form on panel glass).

- Checking energy meters and calibration.

## 5. Annual Activities

- Comprehensive solar system audit by vendor/AMC provider.

- Calibration of inverters and meters as per manufacturer guidelines.

- Preventive replacement of aging connectors, surge arrestors, or fuses.

- Structural inspection of rooftops/grounds to ensure safety.

- Review generation data trends (yearly yield, PR, CUF).

- Verification of compliance with government renewable energy reporting norms.

## 6. Event-Triggered Activities (as needed)

- Emergency inspection after storms, heavy rains, or cyclones.

- Cleaning after bird nesting season, festivals (fireworks dust), or construction dust.

- Immediate troubleshooting if generation drops sharply or error codes are flagged.

## 7. Documentation & Reporting

- Maintain a Solar Energy Generation Register (daily generation, downtime, PR%).

- Record maintenance logs (cleaning, inspection, AMC reports).

- Keep invoices and warranty certificates for all repairs and replacements.

- Submit Monthly Solar Plant Performance Reports to Green Campus Committee & IQAC.

## 8. Accreditation & Standards Linkage

- NAAC Criterion 7 → Institutional Values & Green Campus initiatives.

- NBA Criterion 7 → Facilities & Technical Support (sustainable infrastructure).

- UGC/AICTE Norms → Promotion of renewable energy in campuses.

- NIRF (OI) → Sustainability and inclusivity practices.

- QS I·GAUGE → Green Campus & Energy Efficiency ratings.

- SDGs 7 & 13 → Affordable clean energy, climate action.

# 24. Details of Maintenance on Battery Backups & Inverters

## 1. Daily / Routine Checks

- Visual inspection for physical damage, loose connections, or swelling in batteries.

- Monitor input & output voltage of inverters/UPS.

- Verify indicator lights (charging, battery health, fault) on display panels.

- Record load percentage and ensure it does not exceed recommended levels.

- Ensure inverter/UPS is kept in a dust-free, ventilated environment.

## 2. Weekly Maintenance

- Check electrolyte levels (for lead-acid batteries).

- Inspect cables, fuses, and terminals for corrosion or looseness.

- Ensure proper earthing/grounding of UPS and battery systems.

- Test transfer time (UPS switching to battery mode).

- Confirm fans/cooling systems are operational.

## 3. Monthly Maintenance

- **Battery Testing:**

<!-- -->

- Measure DC voltage & current of each battery.

- Perform load test to check backup duration.

- Monitor internal resistance/impedance values.

<!-- -->

- **Inverter / UPS Testing:**

<!-- -->

- Run inverter/UPS on full load for a short time to confirm backup performance.

- Verify charging system is working correctly.

<!-- -->

- **Cleaning:**

<!-- -->

- Wipe dust from batteries, inverters, and fans.

- Apply petroleum jelly on terminals to prevent corrosion.

<!-- -->

- **Logbook update:**

<!-- -->

- Record battery backup duration, recharge times, and any abnormalities.

## 4. Quarterly Maintenance

- Perform deep discharge and recharge cycle test (if supported).

- Calibrate inverter/UPS meters for accuracy.

- Inspect rack/room ventilation system.

- Check harmonic distortion levels if connected to sensitive lab equipment.

- Update AMC service records with vendor certificates.

## 5. Annual Maintenance

- Comprehensive health check-up of batteries using load banks or vendor-provided diagnostic tools.

- Replace weak or aged batteries (typically 3–5 years lifespan).

- Inspect PCB boards, capacitors, relays, and contactors inside UPS/inverter.

- Review energy efficiency trends (kWh savings vs. grid dependence).

- Validate compliance with ISO/IEC 27001 (IT security power backup) and AICTE/UGC infrastructure norms.

## 6. Event-Triggered Maintenance

- Emergency inspection after power surges, outages, or lightning strikes.

- Post-flood/rain checks for water seepage or short-circuit risks.

- Vendor inspection if sudden drop in backup capacity occurs.

## 7. Documentation & Registers

- **Battery & Inverter Maintenance Register should record:**

<!-- -->

- Date of inspection/servicing.

- Voltage/current readings.

- Backup time logged.

- Issues identified.

- Corrective actions taken.

- AMC/vendor servicing details.

<!-- -->

- Attach service reports, invoices, and photos as audit evidence.

## 8. Accreditation & Standards Linkages

- NAAC Criterion 4 & 7 → Reliable infrastructure & institutional values.

- NBA Criterion 7 → Adequacy of lab/ICT technical support.

- AICTE/UGC Norms → Mandated ICT-enabled infrastructure.

- NIRF (TLR) → Continuous availability of teaching-learning resources.

- QS I·GAUGE → ICT, Green Campus, and Infrastructure compliance.

- ABET Criterion 7 → Adequacy, maintenance, and safety of facilities.

- SDGs 7 & 9 → Affordable clean energy & resilient infrastructure.

# 25. Documentation & Record-Keeping Policy

## 1. Purpose

- To establish a systematic framework for the creation, maintenance, retrieval, and disposal of institutional records.

- To ensure compliance with regulatory, accreditation, and audit requirements.

- To maintain transparency, accountability, and institutional memory.

## 2. Scope

**Applies to all records maintained in the institution, including:**

- Academic Records → student admissions, attendance, examination records, results, degree awards, research publications.

- Administrative Records → HR, payroll, recruitment, meeting minutes, MoUs, policy documents.

- Financial Records → budgets, expenditure reports, AMC agreements, procurement logs.

- IT & Digital Records → IT registers, server logs, ICT facilities, cybersecurity compliance.

- Infrastructure & Maintenance Records → physical facilities, energy, waste, green campus, preventive maintenance registers.

- Accreditation & Compliance Records → NAAC SSR, NBA SAR, NIRF data templates, IQAC reports, SDG mapping.

## 3. Policy Guidelines

### A. Record Creation

- All departments must maintain standardized formats and templates (Registers, Reports, Audit Logs).

- Records must be dated, signed, and verified by designated authority (HOD, Registrar, Dean, IQAC).

- Electronic records should follow a standard naming convention and be stored in structured folders.

### B. Record Maintenance

- Records shall be maintained in both digital and physical formats, wherever applicable.

- Digital copies should be stored on secure servers/cloud with backup.

- Physical files must be stored in fireproof, labeled cabinets in designated record rooms.

- Registers (housekeeping, ICT, facilities, energy, AMC, solar, accreditation) must be updated regularly (daily/weekly/monthly).

### C. Access & Security

- Records are classified as Confidential / Restricted / General.

- Access rights will be role-based (faculty, staff, students, auditors).

- Passwords and digital rights management (DRM) to secure electronic records.

- Confidential records require written approval for access.

### D. Retention & Disposal

- Academic records → retained permanently (admissions, results, degrees).

- Financial records → retained for a minimum of 7 years (as per Income Tax/UGC rules).

- HR records → retained for tenure of employment + 5 years.

- IT & Maintenance records → retained for 3–5 years for audit readiness.

- Disposal of outdated records should follow shredding (physical) or secure deletion (digital) protocols.

### E. Compliance & Audit

- Annual Internal Audit of record-keeping by IQAC/Registrar.

- **Records must be aligned with:**

<!-- -->

- NAAC Criterion 6 (Governance, Leadership & Management).

- NBA Criterion 10 (Continuous Improvement & Record Evidence).

- NIRF Data Submission (Academic, Research, Infrastructure).

- UGC/AICTE Regulations on institutional data management.

- ISO 9001:2015 & ISO 27001:2013 (Quality & Information Security).

## 4. Responsibilities

- Registrar → Custodian of central institutional records.

- HODs / Section Heads → Custodians of departmental records.

- IQAC → Responsible for accreditation documentation, audits, and compliance reporting.

- IT Cell → Secure storage, access control, backup of digital records.

- Facilities Dept. → Maintenance of infrastructure & physical facility registers.

## 5. Monitoring & Review

- Regular quarterly reviews by the Documentation Committee.

- Non-compliance will be reported to the Registrar/IQAC.

- Policy reviewed once every 3 years to ensure alignment with evolving accreditation and regulatory norms.

## 6. Accreditation & Standards Linkages

- NAAC Criterion 6 & 7 → Governance, Management, Best Practices.

- NBA Criterion 10 → Record-keeping as proof of continuous improvement.

- NIRF → Documentation for annual data submission.

- QS I·GAUGE → Governance, Digital Learning Environment, Infrastructure.

- UGC/AICTE → Mandates on record maintenance.

- ABET → Continuous improvement evidence through record-keeping.

- SDGs 16 → Transparency, accountability, and strong institutions.

# 26. Disaster Management & Emergency Response Policy

## 1. Purpose

- To protect the lives of students, faculty, staff, and visitors during disasters and emergencies.

- To safeguard institutional infrastructure, digital assets, and academic continuity.

- To ensure compliance with national disaster management guidelines, higher education regulations, and accreditation frameworks.

## 2. Scope

**Applies to:**

- Natural disasters (earthquakes, floods, cyclones, lightning, fire, pandemics).

- Man-made hazards (chemical spills, lab accidents, electrical failures, IT cyberattacks).

- Campus-specific emergencies (building collapse, riots, food contamination, accidents in playgrounds or hostels).

## 3. Policy Objectives

- Establish an Emergency Response Framework with defined roles and responsibilities.

- Develop Disaster Preparedness Plans (prevention, mitigation, training, drills).

- Ensure continuity of academic, administrative, and IT services.

- Minimize downtime and enable quick recovery.

## 4. Governance Structure

- Disaster Management Committee (DMC) chaired by the Registrar / Principal.

- Emergency Response Teams (ERTs) for fire safety, first aid, evacuation, and IT recovery.

- Departmental Safety Coordinators to implement protocols at department level.

- Liaison Officers to coordinate with local hospitals, police, fire services, and municipal authorities.

## 5. Preparedness Measures

- Risk Assessment & Vulnerability Mapping of campus infrastructure.

- Installation of fire extinguishers, smoke detectors, emergency alarms, and exit signage.

- First Aid Kits & Medical Facilities available in each department, hostel, and playground.

- **Training & Mock Drills:**

<!-- -->

- Fire evacuation drills (half-yearly).

- Earthquake/cyclone response drills (annual).

- Cybersecurity breach simulations (quarterly).

<!-- -->

- **Emergency Communication Systems:**

<!-- -->

- PA systems, SMS/email alerts, WhatsApp groups.

- Emergency helpline numbers displayed across campus.

## 6. Response Protocols

- **Fire & Explosion:** Activate alarms, evacuate, call fire department, use fire extinguishers.

- **Flooding / Water Leakage:** Shut down electrical supply, relocate students/staff, inform municipal authorities.

- **Earthquake:** Drop-Cover-Hold during tremors, assemble at designated safe zones.

- **Medical Emergencies:** First aid, call campus health center/ambulance, record incident.

- **Cybersecurity Incident:** Report to IT Cell, isolate affected systems, activate backup servers.

## 7. Recovery & Continuity

- **Academic Continuity:** Shift to online classes (LMS/ERP) in case of physical disruption.

- **IT Recovery:** Use cloud backups, alternate servers, and VPN-based access.

- **Infrastructure Recovery:** Engineering department to conduct safety inspections and repairs.

- **Counseling & Rehabilitation:** Student counseling, post-disaster stress management.

## 8. Documentation & Reporting

- Maintain a Disaster Incident Register for all events.

- Prepare an Action Taken Report (ATR) after each emergency.

- Submit quarterly preparedness reports to IQAC and Governing Body.

- Store digital evidence (photos, logs, reports) in compliance with NAAC/NBA audits.

## 9. Compliance & Accreditation Linkages

- NAAC Criterion 4 & 7 → Safety, health, and institutional values.

- NBA Criterion 7 → Facilities adequacy & safety preparedness.

- UGC/AICTE Norms → Mandatory fire safety, health, and IT continuity measures.

- NIRF (OI) → Inclusivity, student amenities, campus safety.

- QS I·GAUGE → Safety, governance, and campus resilience metrics.

- ISO 22301:2019 → Business Continuity & Disaster Preparedness standard.

- SDGs 3, 11, 13 → Health, resilient campuses, climate action.

## 10. Responsibilities

- Registrar / Principal → Head of Disaster Management.

- DMC Members → Approve policies, monitor drills, allocate funds.

- ERTs → Fire, medical, evacuation, IT security response.

- HODs → Ensure departmental compliance.

- Students & Staff → Actively participate in training and follow safety protocols.

# 27. Hazardous Materials Handling Policy

## 1. Purpose

- To ensure the safe use, storage, transportation, and disposal of hazardous materials (chemical, biological, radioactive, electronic, biomedical).

- To protect students, staff, faculty, and environment from risks associated with hazardous substances.

- To comply with national regulations, environmental norms, and accreditation standards.

## 2. Scope

Applies to all departments, laboratories, workshops, hostels, and service facilities that handle:

| **Hazard Type**      | **Examples**                                            |
|----------------------|---------------------------------------------------------|
| Chemical hazards     | acids, bases, solvents, reagents, compressed gases.     |
| Biological hazards   | microbial cultures, biomedical waste, sharps.           |
| Electronic hazards   | e-waste, batteries, UPS/inverter chemicals.             |
| Radiological hazards | isotopes (where applicable).                            |
| Waste hazards        | paints, oils, expired lab chemicals, fluorescent lamps. |

## 3. Policy Objectives

- Minimize risks to health, safety, and environment.

- Standardize storage, labeling, and handling procedures.

- Ensure training and awareness among users.

- Establish emergency response mechanisms.

- Integrate hazardous waste management with Green Campus & Sustainability practices.

## 4. Guidelines for Handling

### A. Procurement & Inventory

- Purchase only minimum required quantities.

- Maintain a Hazardous Materials Inventory Register (type, quantity, expiry, storage location).

- Prefer non-hazardous or green alternatives where possible.

### B. Storage

- Store chemicals in designated ventilated rooms with fire protection.

- Separate incompatible chemicals (e.g., acids vs. bases, oxidizers vs. flammables).

- Label all containers with hazard class, expiry date, and responsible person.

- Use secondary containment trays for liquids.

### C. Handling

- Only trained personnel allowed to handle hazardous materials.

- Mandatory use of PPE (gloves, goggles, aprons, respirators).

- Follow Standard Operating Procedures (SOPs) for lab experiments.

- Avoid eating, drinking, or using personal devices in hazardous areas.

### D. Disposal

- Follow Bio-Medical Waste Rules, E-Waste Rules, Hazardous Waste Rules (Govt. of India).

- Segregate waste → Sharps, Chemicals, Solvents, Biologicals, E-waste.

- Use color-coded bins and labels.

- Partner with authorized waste disposal vendors.

### E. Transportation

- Hazardous waste to be transported in sealed containers with proper labeling.

- Maintain Transport Log Register (date, material, qty, vendor, destination).

### F. Emergency Response

- Spill kits available in all hazardous areas.

- First aid kits and emergency showers/eye wash stations in labs.

- Evacuation plan in case of fire, chemical leak, or explosion.

- Incident reporting through Hazardous Incident Register.

## 5. Training & Awareness

- Regular workshops for faculty, staff, and students on hazardous materials handling.

- Emergency response drills (spills, fire, accidents).

- Awareness posters/signage in labs and storage areas.

## 6. Responsibilities

| **Role**                                    | **Responsibility**                                          |
|---------------------------------------------|-------------------------------------------------------------|
| Hazardous Materials Safety Committee (HMSC) | Oversees policy implementation.                             |
| Lab In-Charges / HODs                       | Ensure compliance in their areas.                           |
| Safety Officers                             | Monitor inventory, conduct inspections, maintain registers. |
| Students & Staff                            | Follow guidelines and report unsafe practices.              |

## 7. Documentation

- Hazardous Materials Inventory Register.

- Hazardous Waste Disposal Register.

- Incident & Spill Report Register.

- Training & Safety Drill Records.

## 8. Compliance & Accreditation Linkages

| **Framework**         | **Linkage**                                                                       |
|-----------------------|-----------------------------------------------------------------------------------|
| NAAC Criterion 4 & 7  | Safe learning resources & institutional values.                                   |
| NBA Criterion 7       | Adequacy of lab facilities & safety.                                              |
| UGC/AICTE Norms       | Mandatory lab and workshop safety.                                                |
| NIRF (TLR & OI)       | Infrastructure, inclusivity, sustainability.                                      |
| QS I·GAUGE            | Green campus & safety benchmarks.                                                 |
| ABET Criterion 7      | Facilities adequacy & safety.                                                     |
| ISO 14001 & ISO 45001 | Environmental & Occupational Safety Management.                                   |
| SDGs 3, 6, 11, 12, 13 | Health, clean water, sustainable cities, responsible consumption, climate action. |

## 9. Review & Monitoring

- Quarterly inspections of labs, stores, and workshops.

- Annual Hazardous Materials Audit with compliance reporting.

- Non-compliance reported to Registrar/IQAC.

- Policy reviewed every 3 years for updates.

# 28. Water & Wastewater Management Policy

## 1. Purpose

- To ensure efficient, sustainable, and safe management of water resources within the University.

- To minimize water wastage, promote reuse/recycling, and ensure safe disposal of wastewater.

- To align campus practices with national regulations, accreditation standards, and sustainability goals.

## 2. Scope

**Applicable to:**

- Academic facilities (labs, classrooms, libraries, research centers).

- Student amenities (hostels, canteens, sports complexes, health centers).

- Administrative facilities (departments, offices, exam cells).

- Utilities (water tanks, RO plants, sewage treatment plants, rainwater harvesting).

- Community services (landscaping, irrigation, outreach).

## 3. Objectives

- Ensure equitable access to potable water for students, faculty, and staff.

- Reduce water consumption per capita through conservation measures.

- Treat, recycle, and reuse wastewater for landscaping, flushing, and cooling systems.

- Prevent contamination and pollution of groundwater and nearby water bodies.

- Integrate smart water metering and IoT monitoring for efficient tracking.

## 4. Policy Guidelines

### A. Water Supply & Conservation

- Maintain water meters in all major buildings for consumption monitoring.

- Promote rainwater harvesting across academic and hostel blocks.

- Install low-flow faucets, dual-flush toilets, and sensor taps to reduce wastage.

- Encourage water audits annually to benchmark usage and losses.

### B. Drinking Water

- Provide RO purified / treated drinking water in all academic and hostel areas.

- Regularly monitor water quality (pH, TDS, microbial analysis).

- Maintain a Drinking Water Quality Register for NAAC/NBA evidence.

### C. Wastewater Management

- All wastewater routed through Sewage Treatment Plants (STPs).

- Treated water reused for gardening, flushing, cooling towers.

- Labs and workshops must segregate chemical wastewater and neutralize before disposal.

- Biomedical wastewater disposed as per Bio-Medical Waste Rules.

### D. Rainwater & Stormwater

- Install rainwater harvesting pits in academic, hostel, and sports blocks.

- Prevent stormwater run-off from mixing with sewage lines.

### E. Monitoring & Technology

- Deploy IoT sensors for flow, leakage detection, and quality monitoring.

- Integrate SCADA systems for centralized water usage control.

### F. Awareness & Training

- Conduct water conservation workshops for students and staff.

- Promote “Save Water Campaigns” during NAAC/NIRF events.

- Display posters on water-saving tips across campus.

### G. Emergency Measures

- Maintain backup water supply through storage tanks & borewells.

- Create contingency plan for municipal supply disruptions.

- Regularly inspect pumps, pipelines, and tanks to avoid breakdowns.

## 5. Roles & Responsibilities

- **Water & Wastewater Management Committee (WWMC):** Oversees policy implementation.

- **Facilities / Engineering Department:** Maintenance of supply lines, pumps, and STPs.

- **Green Campus Cell:** Drives sustainability measures and awareness campaigns.

- **Hostel Wardens / HODs:** Ensure departmental-level compliance.

- **Students & Staff:** Follow responsible usage practices.

## 6. Documentation

- Water Consumption Register – Monthly consumption by block/hostel.

- Wastewater Treatment Register – Treated vs. reused vs. discharged.

- Water Quality Reports – Quarterly lab test reports.

- Water Stoppage Register – Incidents and corrective measures.

- Green Audit Reports – Annual sustainability assessment.

## 7. Compliance & Accreditation Linkages

| **Framework**         | **Linkage**                                                                 |
|-----------------------|-----------------------------------------------------------------------------|
| NAAC Criterion 4 & 7  | Infrastructure, Green Campus, Best Practices.                               |
| NBA Criterion 7       | Facilities adequacy, sustainability compliance.                             |
| UGC/AICTE Norms       | Mandatory water safety and STP facilities.                                  |
| NIRF (OI)             | Green initiatives & inclusivity.                                            |
| QS I·GAUGE            | Water management under Green Campus framework.                              |
| ISO 14001 & ISO 46001 | Environmental & water efficiency management.                                |
| SDGs 6, 11, 12, 13    | Clean water, sustainable campuses, responsible consumption, climate action. |

## 8. Review & Monitoring

- Monthly water audit reports submitted to IQAC/Registrar.

- Quarterly wastewater quality monitoring by external agencies.

- Annual Green Audit to include water sustainability metrics.

- Policy to be reviewed every 3 years to integrate new technologies & standards.

- Dual-flush systems installed in 50% of washrooms.

- Rainwater harvesting tanks cleaned before monsoon.

- STP sludge disposal done as per PCB norms.

- **Awareness drive:** *“Save Water – Every Drop Counts.”*

- Introduce IoT-based water monitoring system across campus.

- Increase treated water reuse from 70% → 90% in next 6 months.

- Expand rainwater harvesting to cover Sports Complex roof.

- Integrate Water & Wastewater Dashboard into ERP for IQAC evidence.

| Location                    | Block, hostel, lab, library, canteen, sports facilities.                                                                |
| Reason for Stoppage         | Planned (maintenance, tank cleaning, pipeline inspection) or Unplanned (pump failure, leakage, municipal supply issue). |
| Duration                    | Total downtime in hours/minutes.                                                                                        |
| Description of Impact       | Who/what was affected (hostel bathrooms, canteen food prep, lab experiments).                                           |
| Corrective Actions Taken    | Immediate solutions (repair, alternative water tanker, pump replacement).                                               |
| Responsible Officer / Dept. | Facilities Manager, Hostel Warden, Engineering Dept.                                                                    |
| Verified By                 | Registrar, Safety Committee, IQAC.                                                                                      |
| Remarks                     | Notes on preventive measures, follow-ups, or recurring issues.                                                          |

# 29. Vendor & Contractor Accountability Policy

## 1. Purpose

- To establish a framework of accountability and transparency for all vendors and contractors working with the University.

- To ensure timely, quality, and compliant services in alignment with the University’s vision, sustainability goals, and accreditation requirements.

- To reduce risks of financial mismanagement, safety violations, and environmental non-compliance.

## 2. Scope

- **Applies to all vendors and contractors engaged for:**

<!-- -->

- Civil works (construction, maintenance, renovation).

- Electrical, water, HVAC, and ICT infrastructure.

- Housekeeping, catering, and transport services.

- IT/software, hardware, and digital services.

- Outsourced manpower, security, and AMC (Annual Maintenance Contracts).

## 3. Policy Objectives

- Promote fair vendor selection through transparent procurement and tendering.

- Ensure quality assurance and adherence to standards (safety, green campus, ISO).

- Establish responsibility for delays, damages, and non-compliance.

- Strengthen documentation and record-keeping for audits and accreditation.

- Encourage vendors to adopt sustainable and ethical practices.

## 4. Guidelines

### A. Vendor Selection & Onboarding

- Follow transparent tendering/procurement process as per government/HEI rules.

- Conduct due diligence (past performance, certifications, references).

- Maintain a Vendor Master Register with details (PAN, GST, ISO, compliance records).

- Mandate signing of a Vendor Code of Conduct covering ethics, labor laws, safety, and environment.

### B. Contracts & Agreements

- **All contracts must:**

<!-- -->

- Define scope, deliverables, timelines, penalties.

- Include safety, environmental, and legal compliance clauses.

- Mandate reporting requirements (weekly/monthly status updates).

<!-- -->

- Maintain Contract Compliance Register for monitoring.

### C. Performance Monitoring

- **Use Key Performance Indicators (KPIs) for evaluation:**

<!-- -->

- Timeliness of delivery.

- Quality of work/service.

- Cost adherence.

- Safety compliance (PPE, incident-free work).

- Environmental compliance (waste disposal, use of eco-friendly materials).

<!-- -->

- Conduct quarterly vendor performance reviews.

- Record findings in Vendor Evaluation Reports.

### D. Accountability & Penalties

- **Vendors will be held accountable for:**

<!-- -->

- Delays in delivery or project execution.

- Non-compliance with safety/environmental norms.

- Poor workmanship or substandard material use.

<!-- -->

- **Penalties may include:**

<!-- -->

- Deduction of payment.

- Blacklisting from future contracts.

- Reporting to statutory authorities (if legal violation occurs).

### E. Documentation & Reporting

- **Maintain the following registers:**

<!-- -->

- Vendor Master Register – approved vendors with credentials.

- Contract Compliance Register – contract details, scope, and monitoring.

- Vendor Performance Register – KPIs and periodic evaluation.

- Incident/Non-Compliance Register – safety, quality, or environmental violations.

<!-- -->

- All documents to be digitally stored for IQAC/NAAC/NBA/NIRF evidence.

### F. Sustainability & Green Campus Alignment

- Preference given to vendors using eco-friendly materials, renewable energy, and waste management practices.

- Contractors must align with the University’s Green Campus Policy, Waste Management Policy, and Energy Management Policy.

## 5. Roles & Responsibilities

| **Role**                                 | **Responsibility**                                                                 |
|------------------------------------------|------------------------------------------------------------------------------------|
| Registrar / Finance Officer              | Vendor approval & payments.                                                        |
| Engineering Dept. / IT Cell / Facilities | Technical evaluation of works.                                                     |
| Green Campus Cell                        | Sustainability compliance verification.                                            |
| IQAC                                     | Monitoring vendor accountability evidence for accreditation.                       |
| Vendors / Contractors                    | Deliver services as per contract, with compliance to laws and university policies. |

## 6. Compliance & Accreditation Linkages

| **Framework**          | **Linkage**                                                                         |
|------------------------|-------------------------------------------------------------------------------------|
| NAAC Criterion 6 & 7   | Governance, institutional values, transparency.                                     |
| NBA Criterion 7        | Facilities, maintenance, and compliance processes.                                  |
| UGC/AICTE Norms        | Vendor accountability in procurement and contracts.                                 |
| NIRF (OI)              | Governance and institutional transparency.                                          |
| QS I·GAUGE             | Governance & Sustainability criteria.                                               |
| ISO 9001, 14001, 45001 | Quality, Environmental, and Occupational Health & Safety Management.                |
| SDGs 8, 9, 11, 12      | Decent work, resilient infrastructure, sustainable cities, responsible consumption. |

## 7. Review & Monitoring

- Policy reviewed annually by the Vendor & Contractor Review Committee.

- Audit reports submitted to Governing Body and IQAC.

- Non-compliance cases escalated to Registrar / Principal for action.

# 30. Facility Audit & Inspection Policy

## 1. Purpose

- To ensure that all facilities within the institution are safe, functional, sustainable, and compliant with regulatory, accreditation, and quality standards.

- To provide a systematic mechanism for monitoring, documenting, and improving the physical, ICT, and service-related infrastructure of the University.

- To support continuous improvement in alignment with institutional goals, NAAC/NBA accreditation criteria, and Green Campus commitments.

## 2. Scope

**Applies to all facilities of the University:**

- **Academic:** Classrooms, laboratories, libraries, research centers, smart classrooms.

- **Student Amenities:** Hostels, canteens, health centers, sports complexes, common areas.

- **Administrative:** Offices, exam cells, meeting halls, staff rooms.

- **Digital Infrastructure:** Data centers, ERP/LMS, ICT facilities, Wi-Fi, e-resources.

- **Utilities & Services:** Power, water supply, HVAC, elevators, sanitation.

- **Green & Sustainable Infrastructure:** Solar plants, rainwater harvesting, STPs, waste management units.

## 3. Objectives

- Conduct systematic audits and inspections for safety, functionality, and compliance.

- Identify gaps, risks, and improvement opportunities in facilities management.

- Ensure documentation and evidence-based reporting for accreditation and statutory compliance.

- Promote green, sustainable, and inclusive infrastructure across campus.

- Strengthen student and staff well-being through reliable facilities.

## 4. Audit & Inspection Guidelines

### A. Audit Frequency

| **Frequency**       | **Scope**                                                                                    |
|---------------------|----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------|
| Monthly Inspections | Routine checks (housekeeping, ICT uptime, basic utilities).                                  |
| Quarterly Audits    | Department-level facilities, safety checks, hostel/canteen review.                           |
| Annual Audits       | Comprehensive campus-wide audit (physical facilities, sustainability, ICT, health & safety). |

### B. Audit Parameters

| **Parameter**       | **Coverage**                                                         |
|---------------------|----------------------------------------------------------------------|
| Safety & Compliance | Fire safety, electrical safety, hazardous materials, accessibility.  |
| Functionality       | Working condition of classrooms, labs, ICT systems, furniture.       |
| Sustainability      | Water conservation, renewable energy, waste management, green cover. |
| Accessibility       | Facilities for differently-abled persons (ramps, lifts, signage).    |
| Maintenance Records | AMC, service logs, repair history.                                   |
| User Feedback       | Students, faculty, and staff suggestions/complaints.                 |

### C. Audit Methodology

- Visual inspections and physical verification.

- Checklists for each facility category.

- Review of records (maintenance logs, AMC registers, safety compliance).

- Stakeholder interviews with facility users.

- Rating & scoring system for benchmarking.

### D. Documentation

- Maintain Facility Audit Register with detailed entries.

- Submit Monthly / Quarterly / Annual Facility Audit Reports to IQAC & Governing Body.

- Upload supporting evidence (photos, reports, vendor certificates) in digital repository/ERP.

### E. Corrective & Preventive Action

- Non-compliance → Immediate notification to concerned departments.

- Action Taken Report (ATR) must be submitted within 15 working days.

- Persistent issues escalated to Registrar / Governing Body.

## 5. Roles & Responsibilities

| **Role**                       | **Responsibility**                                             |
|--------------------------------|----------------------------------------------------------------|
| Facility Audit Committee (FAC) | Oversees policy implementation.                                |
| Engineering & Facilities Dept. | Lead audits of physical and utility infrastructure.            |
| IT Cell                        | Audit of ICT facilities and digital systems.                   |
| Green Campus Cell              | Sustainability, energy, and environmental compliance.          |
| IQAC                           | Integration of audit results with accreditation documentation. |
| Students & Staff               | Provide feedback during inspections.                           |

## 6. Compliance & Accreditation Linkages

| **Framework**                  | **Linkage**                                                                                                          |
|--------------------------------|----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------|
| NAAC Criterion 4               | Infrastructure & learning resources.                                                                                 |
| NAAC Criterion 7               | Institutional values & best practices.                                                                               |
| NBA Criterion 7                | Facilities adequacy & technical support.                                                                             |
| UGC/AICTE Norms                | Mandatory safety, ICT, and infrastructure standards.                                                                 |
| NIRF (OI)                      | Infrastructure, inclusivity, and sustainability indicators.                                                          |
| QS I·GAUGE                     | Infrastructure, sustainability, and green campus ratings.                                                            |
| ISO 9001                       | Quality management system.                                                                                           |
| ISO 14001                      | Environmental management system.                                                                                     |
| ISO 45001                      | Health and safety management.                                                                                        |
| SDGs 3, 4, 6, 7, 9, 11, 12, 13 | Health, education, clean water, energy, infrastructure, sustainable cities, responsible consumption, climate action. |

## 7. Review & Monitoring

- Facility audits reviewed at monthly committee meetings.

- Quarterly compliance reports submitted to IQAC.

- Annual audit summary presented to the Governing Body.

- Policy reviewed every 3 years or after major infrastructure expansion.

### Furniture Repair Register

| **Serial Number** | **Furniture Serial Number** | **Repair Date** | **Repair Code (PR = Preventive, CR = Corrective, AMC = Contract-based)** | **Repair Description (Type of work carried out)** | **Quantity Repaired** | **Cost (₹)** | **Responsible Person ID** | **Verified By (Dept. Head / Estate Office / IQAC)** | **Remarks** |
|-------------------|-----------------------------|-----------------|--------------------------------------------------------------------------|---------------------------------------------------|-----------------------|--------------|---------------------------|-----------------------------------------------------|-------------|
|                   |                             |                 |                                                                          |                                                   |                       |              |                           |                                                     |             |

Column Explanation

- Serial Number → Running record of repair activities.

- Furniture Serial Number → Unique ID for each furniture item (e.g., *FUR/2025/01*).

- Repair Date → Actual date of repair.

- **Repair Code → Categorization:**

<!-- -->

- PR = Preventive (routine checks like tightening joints, polishing).

- CR = Corrective (fixing damages like broken legs, re-upholstery).

- AMC = Repairs covered under Annual Maintenance Contract.

<!-- -->

- Repair Description → Details (e.g., *replacement of broken chair legs, polishing of desks, fixing lab stools*).

- Quantity Repaired → Number of items repaired.

- Cost (₹) → Actual expenditure.

- Responsible Person ID → Staff ID of the person overseeing/approving the repair.

- Verified By → Verification by Department Head, Estate Office, or IQAC.

- Remarks → Notes on vendor used, warranty, or follow-up needed.

# 31. Building Safety & Security Policy

## 1. Purpose

- To establish a framework for ensuring safety, security, and risk management of all academic, administrative, residential, and research buildings within the University campus.

- To safeguard students, faculty, staff, visitors, assets, and institutional reputation.

- To comply with national building codes, fire safety standards, and accreditation requirements.

## 2. Scope

- Applies to all academic blocks, hostels, libraries, laboratories, auditoriums, sports complexes, canteens, administrative buildings, and utility structures.

- Covers structural safety, fire safety, electrical safety, physical security, access control, and emergency preparedness.

## 3. Policy Objectives

- Maintain buildings in compliance with NBC, IS Codes, AICTE/UGC norms, and ISO standards.

- Ensure safety audits, preventive maintenance, and risk assessments are conducted regularly.

- Implement modern surveillance and access control systems for campus security.

- Promote a safe, inclusive, and sustainable learning environment.

## 4. Key Provisions

### 4.1 Structural Safety

- All buildings must conform to IS 456 (RCC design), seismic safety norms, and certified structural stability reports.

- Annual structural audits by licensed engineers.

- Preventive maintenance schedules for walls, roofs, flooring, plumbing, and drainage systems.

### 4.2 Fire & Electrical Safety

- **Installation and periodic inspection of:**

<!-- -->

- Fire extinguishers, hydrants, sprinklers, and alarms.

- Emergency exits with clear signage and lighting.

- Lightning arrestors and surge protectors.

<!-- -->

- Fire drills conducted every 6 months.

- Electrical audits conducted annually; faulty wiring and overloading strictly prohibited.

### 4.3 Security & Access Control

- Deployment of trained security personnel with CCTV surveillance covering all critical areas.

- Smart ID-based access for hostels, labs, data centers, and restricted facilities.

- Visitor management system with logbooks and digital records.

- 24×7 campus security helpline and quick response team.

### 4.4 Emergency Preparedness

- Institutional Disaster Management Plan covering fire, earthquake, flood, and health emergencies.

- Emergency evacuation maps displayed in all buildings.

- First-aid kits, stretchers, and trained first responders available at key locations.

- Collaboration with local fire departments, hospitals, and police stations.

### 4.5 Sustainability & Safety

- Integration of green building practices (solar panels, rainwater harvesting, waste management).

- Use of eco-friendly and fire-resistant construction materials.

- Universal accessibility (ramps, lifts, tactile flooring) for persons with disabilities.

## 5. Roles & Responsibilities

- **Registrar / Dean (Planning & Development):** Policy oversight, approvals, and compliance monitoring.

- **Estate & Facilities Department:** Implementation of safety audits, maintenance, and AMC compliance.

- **Safety Officer:** Conducts regular inspections, drills, and incident investigations.

- **IT Cell:** Oversees access control systems, surveillance, and digital monitoring.

- **Heads of Departments:** Ensure departmental compliance with safety protocols.

## 6. Monitoring & Review

- Quarterly safety audits by the Safety Committee.

- Annual Building Safety & Security Report submitted to IQAC, Governing Body, and Accreditation Agencies.

- Regular updates aligned with changes in NBC, UGC/AICTE, and ISO standards.

## 7. Accreditation & Standards Linkages

| **Framework**        | **Linkage**                                                        |
|----------------------|--------------------------------------------------------------------|
| NAAC Criterion 4 & 7 | Infrastructure adequacy, institutional values, and best practices. |
| NBA Criterion 7      | Facility safety and maintenance.                                   |
| UGC/AICTE Norms      | Mandatory fire & building safety certifications.                   |
| NIRF (OI)            | Infrastructure & campus safety.                                    |
| QS I·GAUGE           | Safety and security benchmarks.                                    |
| ISO 45001            | Occupational health & safety management.                           |
| SDGs 3, 4, 11, 13    | Health, quality education, sustainable cities, climate resilience. |

## 5. Risk Assessment & Mitigation

- **High Risks Identified:**

<!-- -->

- Fire extinguisher lapses in classrooms.

- Delayed structural repair in Hostel Block B.

- Access control gaps in Admin Block.

<!-- -->

- **Mitigation Actions:**

<!-- -->

- Renewal of AMC for firefighting equipment.

- Emergency contractor assigned for hostel repairs.

- Proposal for biometric access system in sensitive areas.

## 8. Recommendations

- Immediate renewal of expired firefighting equipment.

- Prioritize hostel structural repairs to avoid safety risks.

- Upgrade CCTV and access control to cover blind spots.

- Conduct a mock evacuation drill in the next quarter.

- Initiate Green & Safe Campus certification to strengthen compliance.
